that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case local-sgd requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l \tau + \mathcal {l} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) \mathcal {l} l}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n \varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l (\tau - 1) \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right)}}{\varepsilon^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

# $\zeta$  -heterogeneous data

applying corollary e.2 and lemma g.5 we get the following result.

theorem g.5. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is  $l$ -smooth for  $i \in [n]$  and  $f_{1},\ldots ,f_{n}$  are  $\zeta$ -heterogeneous and  $\mu$ -strongly convex. let assumption g.1 holds. then local-sgd satisfies assumption 2.3 with

$$
a = 4 \mathcal {l}, \quad b = 0, \quad f = 4 \mathcal {l} l, \quad d _ {1} = 2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + 2 \zeta_ {*} ^ {2},
$$

$$
a ^ {\prime} = \frac {4 \mathcal {l}}{n} + 2 l, \quad b ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad f ^ {\prime} = \frac {4 \mathcal {l} l}{n} + 2 l ^ {2}, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n},
$$

$$
\sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \equiv 0, \quad \rho = 1, \quad c = 0, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0,
$$

$$
h = 0, \quad d _ {3} = 2 (\tau - 1) \left(2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + 2 \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} + \frac {\zeta^ {2}}{\gamma \mu}\right)
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min \left\{\frac {1}{8 \mathcal {l} / n + 4 l}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {2 l \mathcal {l} (\tau - 1)}} \right\}.
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma w _ {k}} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n} + \frac {4 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1)}{\mu} + 8 l (\tau - 1) \gamma \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right)\right).
$$

in particular, if  $\mu > 0$  then

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(\bar {x} ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \leq (1 - \gamma \mu) ^ {k} \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n} + \frac {4 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1)}{\mu} + 8 l (\tau - 1) \gamma \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right)\right) \tag {95}
$$

and when  $\mu = 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(\bar {x} ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \leq \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma k} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n} + \frac {4 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1)}{\mu} + 8 l (\tau - 1) \gamma \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right)\right). \tag {96}
$$

the theorem above together with lemma i.2 implies the following result.

corollary g.9. let assumptions of theorem g.5 hold with  $\mu > 0$ . then for

$$
\begin{array}{l} \gamma_ {0} = \min \left\{\frac {1}{8 \mathcal {l} / _ {n} + 4 l}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {2 l \mathcal {l} (\tau - 1)}} \right\}, \\ \gamma = \min  \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / \left(2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} / n + 4 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) / \mu\right) , \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 8 l (\tau - 1) \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right) \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\} \\ \end{array}
$$

for all  $k$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \text {e i t h e r} \quad \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / \left(2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} / n + 4 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) / \mu\right) , \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 8 l (\tau - 1) \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right) \right\} \right\}\right)}{k} \leq 1 \\ o r \quad \gamma_ {0} \leq \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / \left(2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} / n + 4 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) / \mu\right) , \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 8 l (\tau - 1) \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right) \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \\ \end{array}
$$

we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  is of the order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\left(l + \mathcal {l} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) \mathcal {l} l}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2} \exp \left(- \frac {\mu}{l + \mathcal {l} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) \mathcal {l} l}} k\right) + \frac {\sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n \mu k} + \frac {l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1)}{\mu^ {2} k} + \frac {l (\tau - 1) (\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2})}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right),
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$ . that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^{k}) - f(x^{*})\right]\leq \varepsilon$  in this case local-sgd requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {l}{\mu} + \frac {\mathcal {l}}{n \mu} + \frac {\sqrt {(\tau - 1) \mathcal {l} l}}{\mu} + \frac {\sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \frac {l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1)}{\mu^ {2} \varepsilon} + \sqrt {\frac {l (\tau - 1) (\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2})}{\mu^ {2} \varepsilon}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

combining theorem g.5 and lemma i.3 we derive the following result for the convergence of local-sgd in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary g.10. let assumptions of theorem g.5 hold with  $\mu = 0$ . then for

$$
\begin{array}{l} \gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{8 \mathcal {l} / n + 4 l}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {2 l \mathcal {l} (\tau - 1)}} \right\}, \\ \gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\left(2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} / n + 4 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) / \mu\right) k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{8 l (\tau - 1) \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right) k}} \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$  , we have that

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l + \mathscr {l} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) \mathscr {l} l}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{k} + \sqrt {\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} / n + l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) / \mu\right)}{k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} (\tau - 1) \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right)}}{k ^ {2 / 3}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case local-sgd requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l + \mathcal {l} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) \mathcal {l} l}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\varepsilon} + \frac {\left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} / n + l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) / \mu\right) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l (\tau - 1) \left(\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right)}}{\varepsilon^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

# g.2 local-svrg

as an alternative to local-sgd when the local objective is of a finite sum structure (3), we propose l-svrg [15, 23] stochastic gradient as a local direction instead of the plain stochastic gradient. specifically, we consider

$$
a _ {i} ^ {k} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) + \nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}), \qquad b _ {i} ^ {k} = 0,
$$

where index  $1 \leq j_{i} \leq m$  is selected uniformly at random and  $w_{i}^{k}$  is a particular iterate from the local history updated as follows:

$$
w _ {i} ^ {k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} x _ {i} ^ {k} & \text {w . p .} q \\ w _ {i} ^ {k} & \text {w . p .} 1 - q. \end{array} \right.
$$

next, we will assume that the local functions  $f_{i,j}$  are  $\max l_{ij}$ -smooth.[20] lastly, we will equip the mentioned method with the fixed local loop. the formal statement of the described instance of (4) is given as algorithm 2.

algorithm 2 local-svrg  
require: learning rate  $\gamma >0$  , initial vector  $x^0\in \mathbb{r}^d$  , communication period  $\tau \geq 1$    
1: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do   
2: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do   
3: choose  $j_{i}$  uniformly at random, independently across nodes   
4:  $g_i^k = \nabla f_{i,j_i}(x_i^k) - \nabla f_{i,j_i}(w_i^k) + \nabla f_i(w_i^k)$    
5:  $w_{i}^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x_{i}^{k} & \mathrm{w.p.~}q\\ w_{i}^{k} & \mathrm{w.p.~}1 - q \end{array} \right.$    
6: if  $k + 1$  mod  $\tau = 0$  then   
7:  $x_{i}^{k + 1} = x^{k + 1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\left(x_{i}^{k} - \gamma g_{i}^{k}\right)$    
8: else   
9:  $x_{i}^{k + 1} = x_{i}^{k} - \gamma g_{i}^{k}$    
10: end if   
11: end for   
12: end for

let us next provide the details on the convergence rate. in order to do so, let us identify the parameters of assumption 4.1.

proposition g.1 (see [9]). gradient estimator  $a_{i}^{k}$  satisfies assumption 4.1 with parameters  $a_{i} = 2\max l_{ij}, b_{i} = 2, d_{1,i} = 0, \rho_{i} = q, c_{i} = \max l_{ij}q, d_{2,i} = 0$ , and  $\sigma_{i,k}^2 = \frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|\nabla f_{ij}(w_i^k) - \nabla f_{ij}(x^*)\|^2$ .

# g.2.1  $\zeta$ -heterogeneous data

it remains to use lemma 4.1 along with corollary e.2 to recover all parameters of assumption 2.3 and obtain a convergence rate of algorithm 2 in  $\zeta$ -heterogeneous case.

theorem g.6. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex and  $l$ -smooth for  $i \in [n]$  and  $f_{1},\ldots ,f_{n}$  are  $\zeta$ -heterogeneous, convex and max  $l_{ij}$ -smooth. then local-svrg satisfies assumption 2.3 with

$$
a = 8 \max  l _ {i j}, \quad b = 2, \quad f = 8 l \max  l _ {i j}, \quad d _ {1} = 2 \zeta_ {*} ^ {2},
$$

$$
a ^ {\prime} = \frac {4 \max l _ {i j}}{n} + l, b ^ {\prime} = \frac {1}{n}, f ^ {\prime} = \frac {4 l \max l _ {i j}}{n} + 2 l ^ {2}, d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = 0,
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {4}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \rho = q, \quad c = 8 q \max l _ {i j}, \quad g = 4 q l \max l _ {i j}, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \\ h = \frac {8 (\tau - 1) (2 + q) \gamma^ {2}}{q}, d _ {3} = 2 (\tau - 1) \left(2 \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} + \frac {\zeta^ {2}}{\gamma \mu}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min \left\{\frac {1}{2 \left(^ {4 4 \max l _ {i j} / n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{1 6 \sqrt {l \max l _ {i j} (\tau - 1) (1 + ^ {4 / (1 - q)})}} \right\}.
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma w _ {k}} + 8 l (\tau - 1) \gamma \left(\frac {\zeta^ {2}}{\mu} + 2 \gamma \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right),
$$

where  $\phi^0 = 2\| x^0 -x^*\| ^2 +\frac{8}{3nq}\gamma^2\sigma_0^2 +\frac{32l(\tau - 1)(2 + q)\gamma^3}{q}\sigma_0^2$  in particular, if  $\mu >0$  then

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\gamma \mu , \frac {q}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma} + 8 l (\tau - 1) \gamma \left(\frac {\zeta^ {2}}{\mu} + 2 \gamma \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right) \tag {97}
$$

and when  $\mu = 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(\bar {x} ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \leq \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma k} + 8 l (\tau - 1) \gamma \left(\frac {\zeta^ {2}}{\mu} + 2 \gamma \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}\right). \tag {98}
$$

the theorem above together with lemma i.2 implies the following result.

corollary g.11. let assumptions of theorem g.6 hold with  $\mu > 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min \left\{\frac {1}{2 \left(^ {4 4 \max l _ {i j} / n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{1 6 \sqrt {l \max l _ {i j} (\tau - 1) (1 + ^ {4 / (1 - q)})}} \right\}, q = \frac {1}{m}, m > 1,
$$

$$
\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} = 2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8}{3 n q} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} + \frac {3 2 l (\tau - 1) (2 + q) \gamma_ {0} ^ {3}}{q} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2},
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \min \left\{\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {2} / 8 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) , \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 1 6 l (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\},
$$

for all  $k$  such that

$$
e i t h e r \quad \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {2} / 8 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) , \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 1 6 l (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{k} \leq \frac {1}{m}
$$

$$
o r \qquad \gamma_ {0} \leq \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {2} / 8 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) , \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 1 6 l (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k}
$$

we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  is of the order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \exp \left(- \min \left\{m ^ {- 1}, \gamma_ {0} \mu \right\} k\right) + \frac {\zeta^ {2} l (\tau - 1)}{\mu^ {2} k} + \frac {l (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case local-svrg requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(m + \frac {l}{\mu} + \frac {\max l _ {i j}}{n \mu} + \frac {\sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max l _ {i j}}}{\mu} + \frac {l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1)}{\mu^ {2} \varepsilon} + \sqrt {\frac {l (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}}{\mu^ {2} \varepsilon}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

combining theorem g.6 and lemma i.3 we derive the following result for the convergence of local-svrg in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary g.12. let assumptions of theorem g.6 hold with  $\mu = 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2 \left(^ {4 4 \max  l _ {i j} / n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{1 6 \sqrt {l \max  l _ {i j} (\tau - 1) (1 + ^ {4 / (1 - q)})}} \right\}, \quad q = \frac {1}{m}, \quad m > 1,
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {3 n r _ {0} ^ {2}}{4 m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{1 6 l m (\tau - 1) (2 + 1 / m) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt {\frac {\mu r _ {0} ^ {2}}{4 l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{8 l (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} k}} \right\},
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$ , we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^{k}) - f(x^{*})\right]$  is of the order

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {o} \bigg (\frac {(l + \max l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max l _ {i j}}) r _ {0} ^ {2} + \sqrt {m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2} / n} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{l m (\tau - 1) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{k} \\ \left. + \sqrt {\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2} \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1)}{\mu k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}}}{k ^ {2 / 3}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case local-svrg requires

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {o} \left(\frac {(l + \max l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max l _ {i j}}) r _ {0} ^ {2} + \sqrt {m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2} / n} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{l m (\tau - 1) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{\varepsilon}\right). \\ \left. + \frac {l \zeta^ {2} (\tau - 1) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\mu \varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l (\tau - 1) \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}}}{\varepsilon^ {3 / 2}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

remark g.1. to get the rate from tbl. 4 it remains to apply the following inequality:

$$
\sigma_ {0} ^ {2} = \frac {4}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \stackrel {(6)} {\leq} 4 \max  l _ {i j} ^ {2} \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}.
$$

# g.2.2 heterogeneous data

first of all, we need the following lemma.

lemma g.6. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is  $l$ -smooth for  $i \in [n]$  and  $f_{ij}$  is convex and  $\max l_{ij}$ -smooth for  $i \in [n], j \in [m]$ . then for local-svrg we have

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 6 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 3 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right] + 3 \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}, \tag {99}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \leq 8 \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + \frac {1}{2} \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] + 4 l \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right], \tag {100}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 = \frac{4}{nm}\sum_{i = 1}^n\sum_{j = 1}^m\| \nabla f_{ij}(w_i^k) - \nabla f_{ij}(x^*)\|^2.$

proof. inequality (99) follows from  $\bar{g}_i^k = \mathbf{e}\left[g_i^k\mid x^k\right] = \nabla f_i(x_i^k)$  and inequality (67). next, using young's inequality we derive

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \quad \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 3 6)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i j _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j _ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i j _ {i}} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j _ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - (\nabla f _ {i} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 4 0)} {=} \quad \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i j} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 9), (1 3 9)} {\leq} \frac {4 \max  l _ {i j}}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \right] + \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i j} \left(w _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 3)} {\leq} \quad 8 \max l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} [ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) ] + \frac {1}{2} \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} ] + 4 l \max l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/cdf72bd531e2d65f370941b93463b784f3f23edfbe2be2cf360f27b91fea7728.jpg)

applying corollary e.1, lemma g.6, proposition g.1 and lemma 4.1 we get the following result.

theorem g.7. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex and  $l$ -smooth for  $i \in [n]$  and  $f_{ij}$  is convex and  $\max l_{ij}$ -smooth for  $i \in [n], j \in [m]$ . then local-svrg satisfies assumption e.1 with

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widetilde {a} = 3 l, \quad \widehat {a} = 4 \max l _ {i j}, \quad \widetilde {b} = 0, \quad \widehat {b} = \frac {1}{2}, \quad \widetilde {f} = 3 l ^ {2}, \quad \widehat {f} = 4 l \max l _ {i j}, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = 3 \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}, \quad \widehat {d} _ {1} = 0 \\ a ^ {\prime} = \frac {4 \max l _ {i j}}{n} + l, \quad b ^ {\prime} = \frac {1}{n}, \quad f ^ {\prime} = \frac {4 l \max l _ {i j}}{n} + 2 l ^ {2}, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = 0, \\ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {4}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (w _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad \rho = q, \quad c = 8 q \max l _ {i j}, \quad g = 4 q l \max l _ {i j}, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \\ h = \frac {2 e (\tau - 1) (2 + q) \gamma^ {2}}{q}, d _ {3} = 6 e (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

with  $\gamma$  satisfying

$$
\gamma \leq \min \left\{\frac {1}{2 \left(^ {4 4 \max l _ {i j} / n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{4 \sqrt {2 e l (\tau - 1) (3 l (\tau - 1) + 4 \max l _ {i j} + ^ {8 \max l _ {i j} / (1 - q)})}} \right\}.
$$

and for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma w _ {k}} + 2 4 e l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \gamma^ {2},
$$

where  $\phi^0 = 2\| x^0 -x^*\| ^2 +\frac{8}{3nq}\gamma^2\sigma_0^2 +\frac{8el(\tau - 1)(2 + q)\gamma^3}{q}\sigma_0^2$  in particular, if  $\mu >0$  then

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(\bar {x} ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\gamma \mu , \frac {q}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma} + 2 4 e l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \gamma^ {2} \tag {101}
$$

and when  $\mu = 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(\bar {x} ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \leq \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma k} + 2 4 e l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \gamma^ {2}. \tag {102}
$$

the theorem above together with lemma i.2 implies the following result.

corollary g.13. let assumptions of theorem g.7 hold with  $\mu > 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{2 \left(^ {4 4 \max  l _ {i j} / n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{4 \sqrt {2 e l (\tau - 1) (3 l (\tau - 1) + 4 \max  l _ {i j} + ^ {8 \max  l _ {i j} / (1 - q)})}} \right\},
$$

$$
\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} = 2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {8}{3 n q} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} + \frac {8 e l (\tau - 1) (2 + q) \gamma_ {0} ^ {3}}{q} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}, \quad q = \frac {1}{m}, \quad m > 1,
$$

$$
\gamma = \min  \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \frac {\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 2 4 e l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \}}{\mu k}\right)\right)}{\mu k} \right\},
$$

for all  $k$  such that

$$
\text {e i t h e r} \quad \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 2 4 e l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \right\}\right)}{k} \leq \frac {1}{m} \quad o r \quad \gamma_ {0} \leq \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 2 4 e l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} \right\}\right)}{\mu k}
$$

we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  is of the order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \exp \left(- \min  \left\{m ^ {- 1}, \gamma_ {0} \mu \right\} k\right) + \frac {l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case local-svrg requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(m + \frac {l \tau}{\mu} + \frac {\max l _ {i j}}{n \mu} + \frac {\sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max l _ {i j}}}{\mu} + \sqrt {\frac {l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}}{\mu^ {2} \varepsilon}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

combining theorem g.7 and lemma i.3 we derive the following result for the convergence of local-svrg in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary g.14. let assumptions of theorem g.7 hold with  $\mu = 0$ . then for  $q = \frac{1}{m}$ ,  $m > 1$  and

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min \left\{\frac {1}{2 \left(^ {4 4 \max l _ {i j} / n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{4 \sqrt {2 e l (\tau - 1) (3 l (\tau - 1) + 4 \max l _ {i j} + ^ {8 \max l _ {i j} / (1 - q)})}} \right\},
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {3 n r _ {0} ^ {2}}{4 m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{4 e l m (\tau - 1) (2 + 1 / m) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{1 2 e l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2} k}} \right\},
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$ , we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  is of the order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {(l \tau + \max l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max l _ {i j}}) r _ {0} ^ {2} + \sqrt {m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2} / n} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{l m (\tau - 1) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{k} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}}}{k ^ {2 / 3}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case local-svrg requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {(l \tau + \max l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max l _ {i j}}) r _ {0} ^ {2} + \sqrt {m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2} / n} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{l m (\tau - 1) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{\varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l (\tau - 1) ^ {2} \zeta_ {*} ^ {2}}}{\varepsilon^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

remark g.2. to get the rate from tbl. 4 it remains to apply the following inequality:

$$
\sigma_ {0} ^ {2} = \frac {4}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \stackrel {(6)} {\leq} 4 \max l _ {i j} ^ {2} \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}.
$$

# g.3 s*-local-sgd

in this section we consider the same settings as in section g.1.1 and our goal is to remove one of the main drawbacks of local-sgd in heterogeneous case which in the case of  $\mu$ -strongly convex  $f_{i}$  with  $\mu > 0$  converges with linear rate only to the neighbourhood of the solution even in the full-gradients case, i.e. when  $d_{1,i} = 0$  for all  $i \in [n]$ . however, we start with unrealistic assumption that  $i$ -th node has an access to  $\nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$  for all  $i \in [n]$ . under this assumption we present a new method called star-shifted local-sgd (s*-local-sgd, see algorithm 3).

lemma g.7. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ . then for all  $k \geq 0$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \mid x _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right), \tag {103}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 l ^ {2} v _ {k}, \tag {104}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x _ {i} ^ {k} \right] \leq \sigma^ {2}, \tag {105}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \leq 4 l \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 l ^ {2} v _ {k} + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n}, \tag {106}
$$

where  $\sigma^2 \stackrel{def}{=} \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} d_{1,i}$  and  $\mathbf{e}[\cdot \mid x^k] \stackrel{def}{=} \mathbf{e}[\cdot \mid x_1^k, \ldots, x_n^k]$ .

algorithm 3 s*-local-sgd  
require: learning rate  $\gamma >0$  , initial vector  $x^0\in \mathbb{r}^d$  , communication period  $\tau \geq 1$    
1: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do   
2: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do   
3: sample  $\hat{g}_i^k = \nabla f_{\xi_i^k}(x_i^k)$  independently from other nodes   
4:  $g_{i}^{k} = \hat{g}_{i}^{k} - \nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$    
5: if  $k + 1$  mod  $\tau = 0$  then   
6:  $x_{i}^{k + 1} = x^{k + 1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\left(x_{i}^{k} - \gamma g_{i}^{k}\right)$  ▷ averaging   
7: else   
8:  $x_{i}^{k + 1} = x_{i}^{k} - \gamma g_{i}^{k}$  ▷ local update   
9: end if   
10: end for   
11: end for

proof. first of all, we notice that  $\mathbf{e}\left[g_i^k\mid x_i^k\right] = \nabla f_i(x_i^k) - \nabla f_i(x^*)$  and

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \mid x _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right).
$$

using this we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \stackrel {(1 9)} {\leq} \frac {2 l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} d _ {f _ {i}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}) \\ \stackrel {(6 3)} {\leq} 4 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + 2 l ^ {2} v _ {k} \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \mid x _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {(6 6)} {\leq} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} d _ {1, i} =: \sigma^ {2}.
$$

finally, using independence of  $g_1^k, g_2^k, \ldots, g_n^k$  and  $\frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \nabla f_i(x^*) = \nabla f(x^*) = 0$  we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] \stackrel {(1 3 9) _ {\underline {{=}}} (1 0 3)} {=} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(g _ {i} ^ {k} - \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \mid x ^ {k} \right] + \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \\ = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k})\right) \right\| ^ {2} | x ^ {k} \right] + \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \\ = \quad \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] + \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(6 6), (6 8)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + 2 l ^ {2} v _ {k} + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

applying corollary e.1 and lemma g.7 we get the following result.

theorem g.8. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex and  $l$ -smooth for every  $i \in [n]$ . then s*-local-sgd satisfies assumption e.1 with

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widetilde {a} = 2 l, \quad \widehat {a} = 0, \quad \widetilde {b} = \widehat {b} = 0, \quad \widetilde {f} = 2 l ^ {2}, \quad \widehat {f} = 0, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = 0, \quad \widehat {d} _ {1} = \sigma^ {2} := \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} d _ {1, i} \\ a ^ {\prime} = 2 l, \quad b ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad f ^ {\prime} = 2 l ^ {2}, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n}, \quad \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \equiv 0, \quad \rho = 1, \quad c = 0, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \\ h = 0, \quad d _ {3} = 2 e (\tau - 1) \sigma^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

consequently, if

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {e} (\tau - 1) l} \right\}.
$$

we have for  $\mu >0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq (1 - \gamma \mu) ^ {k} \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n} + 4 e l (\tau - 1) \gamma \sigma^ {2}\right)
$$

and when  $\mu = 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma k} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n} + 4 e l (\tau - 1) \gamma \sigma^ {2}\right).
$$

in the special case when  $\nabla f_{\xi_i^k}(x_i^k) = \nabla f_i(x_i^k)$  for all  $i \in [n]$  and  $k \geq 0$  we obtain s*-local-gd which converges with  $\mathcal{o}\left(\tau \kappa \ln \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)$  rate when  $\mu > 0$  and with  $\mathcal{o}\left(\frac{l\tau\|x^0 - x^*\|^2}{\varepsilon}\right)$  rate when  $\mu = 0$  to the exact solution asymptotically. the theorem above together with lemma i.2 implies the following result.

corollary g.15. let assumptions of theorem g.8 hold with  $\mu > 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {e} (\tau - 1) l}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \min \left\{\| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} n \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / \sigma^ {2} , \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 4 e l (\tau - 1) \sigma^ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}
$$

for all  $k$  such that

either  $\frac{\ln\left(\max\left\{2,\min\left\{\|x^0 - x^*\|^2n\mu^2k^2 / \sigma^2,\|x^0 - x^*\|^2\mu^3k^3 / 4el(\tau - 1)\sigma^2\right\} \right\}\right)}{k}\leq 1$

or  $\min \left\{\frac{1}{4l},\frac{1}{8\sqrt{e}(\tau - 1)l}\right\} \leq \frac{\ln\left(\max\left\{2,\min\left\{\|x^0 - x^*\|^2n\mu^2k^2/\sigma^2,\|x^0 - x^*\|^2\mu^3k^3/4el(\tau - 1)\sigma^2\right\}\right)}{\mu k}$

we have that

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\tau l \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \exp \left(- \frac {\mu}{\tau l} k\right) + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n \mu k} + \frac {l (\tau - 1) \sigma^ {2}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case s*-local-sgd requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\tau l}{\mu} + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \sqrt {\frac {l (\tau - 1) \sigma^ {2}}{\mu^ {2} \varepsilon}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

combining theorem g.8 and lemma i.3 we derive the following result for the convergence of s*-local-sgd in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary g.16. let assumptions of theorem g.8 hold with  $\mu = 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {e} (\tau - 1) l}, \sqrt {\frac {n r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2}}{4 e l (\tau - 1) \sigma^ {2} k}} \right\},
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$  , we have that

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\tau l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{k} + \sqrt {\frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sigma^ {2}}{n k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} (\tau - 1) \sigma^ {2}}}{k ^ {2 / 3}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case s*-local-sgd requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\tau l r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\varepsilon} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sigma^ {2}}{n \varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l (\tau - 1) \sigma^ {2}}}{\varepsilon^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

# g.4 ss-local-sgd

# g.4.1 uniformly bounded variance

in this section we consider the same settings as in section g.1.1

algorithm 4 stochastically shifted local-sgd (ss-local-sgd)  
require: learning rate  $\gamma >0$  , initial vector  $x^0\in \mathbb{r}^d$  , probability of communication  $p\in (0,1]$  , probability of the shift's update  $q\in (0,1]$  , batchsize  $r$  for computing shifts   
1:  $y^{0} = x^{0}$    
2: for  $i\in [n]$  compute  $r$  independent samples  $\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_{i,1}}^{0}(y^{0}),\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_{i,2}}^{0}(y^{0}),\ldots ,\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_{i,r}}^{0}(y^{0})$  , set  $\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_i^0}(y^0) =$ $\frac{1}{r}\sum_{j = 1}^{r}\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_{i,j}}^{0}(y^{0})$  and  $\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_i^0}(y^0) = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_i^0}(y^0)$    
3: for  $k = 0,1,\dots$  do   
4: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do   
5: sample  $\nabla f_{\xi_i^k}(x_i^k)$  independently from other nodes   
6:  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{\xi_{i}^{k}}(x_{i}^{k}) - \nabla f_{\widetilde{\xi}_{i}^{k}}(y^{k}) + \nabla f_{\widetilde{\xi}_{i}^{k}}(y^{k})$  , where  $\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_i^k}(y^k) = \frac{1}{r}\sum_{j = 1}^{r}\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_{i,j}^k}(y^k)$  and  $\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}^k}(y^k) =$ $\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\nabla f_{\bar{\xi}_i^k}(y^k)$    
7:  $x_{i}^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^{k + 1}, & \mathrm{w.p.~}p,\\ x_{i}^{k} - \gamma g_{i}^{k}, & \mathrm{w.p.~}1 - p, \end{array} \right.$  where  $x^{k + 1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}(x_i^k -\gamma g_i^k)$    
8:  $y^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^k,\quad \mathrm{w.p.~}q,\\ y^k,\quad \mathrm{w.p.~}1 - q, \end{array} \right.$  and for all  $i\in [n], j\in [r]$ $\overline{\xi}_{i,j}^{k + 1}$  is a fresh sample, if  $y^{k + 1}\neq y^k$  equal to  $\overline{\xi}_{i,j}^k$  otherwise.   
9: end for   
10: end for

the main algorithm in this section is stochastically shifted local-sgd (ss-local-svrg, see algorithm 4). we notice that the updates for  $x_{i}^{k + 1}$  and  $y^{k + 1}$  can be dependent, e.g., one can take  $p = q$  and update  $y^{k + 1}$  as  $x^{k}$  every time  $x_{i}^{k + 1}$  is updated by  $x^{k + 1}$ . moreover, with probability  $q$  line 8 implies a round of communication and computation of new stochastic gradient by each worker.

we emphasize that in expectation  $y^{k}$  is updated only once per  $\lceil 1 / q \rceil$  iterations. therefore, if  $r = o(1 / q)$  and  $q \leq p$ , then up to a constant numerical factor the overall expected number of oracle calls and communication rounds are the same as for local-sgd with either the same probability  $p$  of communication or with constant local loop length  $\tau = \lceil 1 / p \rceil$ .

finally, we notice that due to independence of  $\overline{\xi}_{i,1}^k,\overline{\xi}_{i,2}^k,\ldots ,\overline{\xi}_{i,r}^k$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {k}} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \stackrel {(6 6)} {\leq} \frac {d _ {1 , i}}{r}. \tag {107}
$$

lemma g.8. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ . then for all  $k \geq 0$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right), \tag {108}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 8 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] + 4 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right] + \frac {2 \sigma^ {2}}{r}, \tag {109}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq \sigma^ {2}, \tag {110}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \leq 4 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right] + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n}, \tag {111}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 \stackrel{def}{=} \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \left\| \nabla f_i(y^k) - \nabla f_i(x^*) \right\|^2$  and  $\sigma^2 \stackrel{def}{=} \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} d_{1,i}$ .

proof. we start with unbiasedness:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {\overline {{\xi}} _ {i} ^ {k}} \left(y ^ {k}\right) + \nabla f _ {\overline {{\xi}} ^ {k}} \left(y ^ {k}\right) \right] \\ { = } { \frac { 1 } { n } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n } \mathbf { e } _ { k } \left[ \nabla f _ { \xi _ { i } ^ { k } } ( x _ { i } ^ { k } ) \right] + \mathbf { e } _ { k } \left[ \nabla f _ { \bar { \xi } ^ { k } } ( y ^ { k } ) - \frac { 1 } { n } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n } \nabla f _ { \bar { \xi } _ { i } ^ { k } } ( y ^ { k } ) \right] = \frac { 1 } { n } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n } \nabla f _ { i } ( x _ { i } ^ { k } ) . } \\ \end{array}
$$

using this we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \quad \stackrel {(1 3 6)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - \left(\nabla f _ {\xi^ {k}} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 9), (1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {4 l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \right] + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {k}} \left(y ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 3), (1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad 8 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 4 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right] + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {i} \left(y ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {k}} \left(y ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(y ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 0 7)} {\leq} \quad 8 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + 2 \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} ] + 4 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ] + \frac {2 \sigma^ {2}}{r} \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {(6 6)} {\leq} \sigma^ {2}.
$$

finally, we use independence of  $\nabla f_{\xi_n^k}(x_1^k),\ldots ,\nabla f_{\xi_n^k}(x_n^k)$  and derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {=} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \right] + \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 8)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + 2 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ] + \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 6)} {\leq} \quad 4 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + 2 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right] + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n} \\ \end{array}
$$

which finishes the proof.

lemma g.9. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ . then for all  $k \geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \right] \leq (1 - q) \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] + 2 l q \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \tag {112}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \left\| \nabla f_i(y^k) - \nabla f_i(x^*) \right\|^2$ .

proof. by definition of  $y^{k + 1}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x _ {1} ^ {k}, \dots , x _ {n} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1 - q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} + \frac {q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 9)} {\leq} \quad (1 - q) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 l q (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})). \\ \end{array}
$$

taking the full mathematical expectation on both sides of previous inequality and using the tower property (140) we get the result.

using corollary e.3 we obtain the following theorem.

theorem g.9. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex and  $l$ -smooth for every  $i \in [n]$ . then ss-local-sgd satisfies assumption e.1 with

$$
\widetilde {a} = 4 l, \quad \widehat {a} = 0, \quad \widetilde {b} = 2, \quad \widehat {b} = 0, \quad \widetilde {f} = 4 l ^ {2}, \quad \widehat {f} = 0, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = \frac {2 \sigma^ {2}}{r}, \quad \widehat {d} _ {1} = \sigma^ {2}, \quad \sigma^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} d _ {1, i},
$$

$$
a ^ {\prime} = 2 l, \quad b ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad f ^ {\prime} = 2 l ^ {2}, \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n},
$$

$$
\sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left| \left| \nabla f _ {i} \left(y ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right| \right| ^ {2}, \quad \rho = q, \quad c = l q, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0,
$$

$$
h = \frac {1 2 8 (1 - p) (2 + p) (2 + q) \gamma^ {2}}{3 p ^ {2} q}, d _ {3} = \frac {8 (1 - p)}{p ^ {2}} \left(\frac {2 (p + 2) \sigma^ {2}}{r} + p \sigma^ {2}\right)
$$

under assumption that

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 l \sqrt {2 (1 - p) (2 + p) (1 + ^ {1 / (1 - q)})}} \right\}.
$$

moreover, for  $\mu > 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min \left\{\gamma \mu , \frac {q}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n} + \gamma \frac {1 6 l (1 - p)}{p ^ {2}} \left(\frac {2 (p + 2) \sigma^ {2}}{r} + p \sigma^ {2}\right)\right)
$$

and when  $\mu = 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma k} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n} + \gamma \frac {1 6 l (1 - p)}{p ^ {2}} \left(\frac {2 (p + 2) \sigma^ {2}}{r} + p \sigma^ {2}\right)\right)
$$

where  $\phi^0 = 2\| x^0 -x^*\| ^2 +\frac{512l(1 - p)(2 + p)(2 + q)\gamma^3\sigma_0^2}{3p^2q}$

the theorem above together with lemma i.2 implies the following result.

corollary g.17. let assumptions of theorem g.9 hold with  $\mu > 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 l \sqrt {2 (1 - p) (2 + p) \left(1 + ^ {1 / (1 - q)}\right)}} \right\},
$$

$$
\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} = 2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {5 1 2 l (1 - p) (2 + p) (2 + q) \gamma_ {0} ^ {3} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}{3 p ^ {2} q}, \quad q = p,
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \min \left\{n \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / 2 \sigma^ {2} , p \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 3 2 l (1 - p) (3 p + 4) \sigma^ {2} \right\}\right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}, \quad r = \left\lceil \frac {1}{p} \right\rceil ,
$$

for all  $k$  such that

$$
\text {e i t h e r} \quad \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{n \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / 2 \sigma^ {2} , p \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 3 2 l (1 - p) (3 p + 4) \sigma^ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{k} \leq p
$$

$$
o r \quad \gamma_ {0} \leq \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{n \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / 2 \sigma^ {2} , p \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 3 2 l (1 - p) (3 p + 4) \sigma^ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k}
$$

we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  is of the order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {1}{p}, \gamma_ {0} \mu \right\} k\right) + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n \mu k} + \frac {l (1 - p) \sigma^ {2}}{p \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case ss-local-sgd requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {l}{p \mu} + \frac {\sigma^ {2}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \sqrt {\frac {l (1 - p) \sigma^ {2}}{p \mu^ {2} \varepsilon}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node (in expectation) and  $1/p$  times less communication rounds.

combining theorem g.9 and lemma i.3 we derive the following result for the convergence of ss-local-sgd in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary g.18. let assumptions of theorem g.9 hold with  $\mu = 0$ . then for  $q = p$ ,  $r = \lceil 1 / p \rceil$  and

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 l}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 l \sqrt {2 (1 - p) (2 + p) (1 + ^ {1} / (1 - q))}} \right\},
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {3 p ^ {3} r _ {0} ^ {2}}{2 5 6 l (1 - p) (2 + p) ^ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt {\frac {n r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2} k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {p r _ {0} ^ {2}}{1 6 l (1 - p) (3 p + 4) \sigma^ {2} k}} \right\},
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$ , we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  is of the order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{l (1 - p) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{p k} + \sqrt {\frac {\sigma^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2}}{n k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} (1 - p) \sigma^ {2}}}{p ^ {1 / 3} k ^ {2 / 3}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case ss-local-sgd requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {l r _ {0} ^ {2} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{l (1 - p) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{p \varepsilon} + \frac {\sigma^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2}}{n \varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l (1 - p) \sigma^ {2}}}{p ^ {1 / 2} \varepsilon^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node (in expectation) and  $1/p$  times less communication rounds.

remark g.3. to get the rate from tbl. 4 it remains to apply the following inequality:

$$
\sigma_ {0} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \stackrel {(6)} {\leq} l ^ {2} \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}.
$$

# g.4.2 expected smoothness and arbitrary sampling

in this section we consider the same method ss-local-sgd, but without assumption that the stochastic gradient has a uniformly bounded variance. instead of this we consider the same setup as in section g.1.2, i.e. we assume that each worker  $i \in [n]$  at any point  $x \in \mathbb{r}^d$  has an access to the unbiased estimator  $\nabla f_{\xi_i}(x)$  of  $\nabla f_i(x)$  satisfying assumption g.1.

lemma g.10. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ . let assumption g.1 holds. then for all  $k \geq 0$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right), \tag {113}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 8 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] + 4 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right], \tag {114}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 8 \mathcal {l} \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 4 \mathcal {l} l \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right] + 2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}, \tag {115}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \leq 4 \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 l \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right] + \frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n}, \tag {116}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 \stackrel{def}{=} \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \left\| \nabla f_{\xi_i^k}(y^k) - \nabla f_i(x^*) \right\|^2$  and  $\sigma_*^2 \stackrel{def}{=} \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \mathbf{e}_{\xi_i} \| \nabla f_{\xi_i}(x^*) - \nabla f_i(x^*) \|^2$ .

proof. first of all, (113) follows from (108). next, using  $\bar{g}_i^k = \nabla f_i(x_i^k) - \nabla f_{\overline{\xi}_i^k}(y^k) + \nabla f_{\overline{\xi}^k}(y^k)$  we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \quad \stackrel {(1 3 6)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {\overline {{\xi}} _ {i} ^ {k}} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - (\nabla f _ {\overline {{\xi}} ^ {k}} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f (x ^ {*})) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 9), (1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {4 l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \right] + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {k}} \left(y ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 3)} {\leq} \quad 8 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + 2 \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} ] + 4 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ] \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \quad = \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 6)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(8 6)} {\leq} \quad \frac {4 \mathcal {l}}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(6 3)} {\leq} \quad 8 \mathcal {l} \mathbf {e} [ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) ] + 4 \mathcal {l} l \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ] + 2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}. \tag {117} \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, we use independence of  $\xi_1^k,\ldots ,\xi_n^k$  and derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \quad = \quad \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 4 0) _ {,} (1 3 9)} {=} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right)\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] + \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ = \quad \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] + \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 1 7), (6 8)} {\leq} \quad 4 \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + 2 l \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ] + \frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n} \\ \end{array}
$$

which finishes the proof.

lemma g.11. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$  and assumption g.1 holds. then for all  $k \geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \right] \leq (1 - q) \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] + 2 q \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{r} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + \frac {2 q \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{r} \tag {118}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \left\| \nabla f_{\xi_i^k}(y^k) - \nabla f_i(x^*) \right\|^2$  and  $\sigma_*^2 \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \mathbf{e}_{\xi_i} \| \nabla f_{\xi_i}(x^*) - \nabla f_i(x^*) \|^2$ .

proof. by definition of  $y^{k + 1}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x _ {1} ^ {k}, \dots , x _ {n} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1 - q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {\vec {\xi} _ {i} ^ {k}} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {k + 1}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {k + 1}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {=} \quad (1 - q) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + \frac {q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ + \frac {q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\overline {{\xi}} _ {i} ^ {k + 1}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\overline {{\xi}} _ {i} ^ {k + 1}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

next, we use independence of  $\overline{\xi}_{i,1}^{k + 1},\overline{\xi}_{i,2}^{k + 1},\ldots ,\overline{\xi}_{i,r}^{k + 1}$  for all  $i\in [n]$  and derive

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \mid x _ {1} ^ {k}, \dots , x _ {n} ^ {k} \right] \quad = \quad (1 - q) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + \frac {q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}
$$

$$
+ \frac {q}{n r ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {r} \mathbf {e} _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j} ^ {k + 1}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j} ^ {k + 1}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) \| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 9), (1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad (1 - q) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 l q \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \\ + \frac {q}{n r ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {r} \mathbf {e} _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j} ^ {k + 1}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j} ^ {k + 1}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 3 6)} {\leq} \quad (1 - q) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 l q \left(f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \\ + \frac {2 q}{n r ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {r} \mathbf {e} _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j}} ^ {k + 1} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j} ^ {k + 1}} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j} ^ {k + 1}} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2 q}{n r ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {r} \mathbf {e} _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j} ^ {k + 1}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i, j} ^ {k + 1}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\stackrel {(8 6)} {\leq} \qquad (1 - q) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 q \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{r} + l\right) \left(f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})\right) + \frac {2 q \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{r}.
$$

taking the full mathematical expectation on both sides of previous inequality and using the tower property (140) we get the result.

using corollary e.3 we obtain the following theorem.

theorem g.10. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex and  $l$ -smooth for every  $i \in [n]$ . let assumption g.1 holds. then ss-local-sgd satisfies assumption e.1 with

$$
\widetilde {a} = 4 l, \quad \widehat {a} = 4 \mathcal {l}, \quad \widetilde {b} = 2, \quad \widehat {b} = 0, \quad \widetilde {f} = 4 l ^ {2}, \quad \widehat {f} = 4 \mathcal {l} l, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = 0,
$$

$$
\widehat {d} _ {1} = 2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}, \quad \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i}} \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2}, \quad a ^ {\prime} = 2 \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right), \quad b ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad f ^ {\prime} = 2 l \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right),
$$

$$
d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n}, \quad \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i} ^ {k}} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2}, \quad \rho = q, \quad c = q \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{r} + l\right), \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = \frac {2 q \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{r},
$$

$$
h = \frac {1 2 8 (1 - p) (2 + p) (2 + q) \gamma^ {2}}{3 p ^ {2} q}, d _ {3} = \frac {8 (1 - p)}{p ^ {2}} \left(2 p \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \frac {3 2 (2 + p) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{3 r}\right)
$$

underassumptionthat

$$
\gamma \leq \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right)}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 \sqrt {2 l (1 - p) \left((2 + p) l + p \mathcal {l} + \frac {(2 + p) (2 \mathcal {l} / r + l)}{(1 - q)}\right)}} \right\}.
$$

moreover, for  $\mu > 0$  we have

$$
{\bf e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min  \left\{\gamma \mu , \frac {q}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n} + \gamma \frac {1 6 l (1 - p)}{p ^ {2}} \left(2 p \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \frac {3 2 (2 + p) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{3 r}\right)\right)
$$

and when  $\mu = 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {\phi^ {0}}{\gamma k} + 2 \gamma \left(\frac {2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n} + \gamma \frac {1 6 l (1 - p)}{p ^ {2}} \left(2 p \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} + \frac {3 2 (2 + p) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{3 r}\right)\right)
$$

where  $\phi^0 = 2\| x^0 -x^*\| ^2 +\frac{512l(1 - p)(2 + p)(2 + q)\gamma^3{\bf e}[\sigma_0^2]}{3p^2q}$

the theorem above together with lemma i.2 implies the following result.

corollary g.19. let assumptions of theorem g.10 hold with  $\mu > 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right)}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 \sqrt {2 l (1 - p) \left((2 + p) l + p \mathcal {l} + \frac {(2 + p) (2 \mathcal {l} / r + l)}{(1 - q)}\right)}} \right\},
$$

$$
\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} = 2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {5 1 2 l (1 - p) (2 + p) (2 + q) \gamma_ {0} ^ {3} \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} ]}{p ^ {2} q}, q = p,
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \frac {\ln \left(\max \left\{2 , \min \left\{n \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / 4 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} , p \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 6 4 l (1 - p) (1 + 3 2 (2 + p) / 3) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} \right\}\right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\}, \quad r = \left\lceil \frac {1}{p} \right\rceil ,
$$

for all  $k$  such that

$$
{ e i t h e r } { \frac { \ln \left( \operatorname* { m a x } \left\{ 2 , \operatorname* { m i n } \left\{ n \widetilde { \phi } ^ { 0 } \mu ^ { 2 } k ^ { 2 } / 4 \sigma _ { * } ^ { 2 } , p \widetilde { \phi } ^ { 0 } \mu ^ { 3 } k ^ { 3 } / 6 4 l ( 1 - p ) ( 1 + ^ { 3 2 ( 2 + p ) / 3 } ) \sigma _ { * } ^ { 2 } \right\} \right\} \right) } { k } \leq p }
$$

$$
o r \quad \gamma_ {0} \leq \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{n \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / 4 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} , p \widetilde {\phi} ^ {0} \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / 6 4 l (1 - p) (1 + ^ {3 2 (2 + p)} / 3) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k}
$$

we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  is of the order

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {\widetilde {\phi} ^ {0}}{\gamma_ {0}} \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {1}{p}, \gamma_ {0} \mu \right\} k\right) + \frac {\sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n \mu k} + \frac {l (1 - p) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{p \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case ss-local-sgd requires

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {l}{p \mu} + \frac {\mathcal {l}}{n \mu} + \frac {\sqrt {\mathcal {l} l (1 - p)}}{\sqrt {p} \mu} + \frac {\sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{n \mu \varepsilon} + \sqrt {\frac {l (1 - p) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}{p \mu^ {2} \varepsilon}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node (in expectation) and  $1/p$  times less communication rounds.

combining theorem g.10 and lemma i.3 we derive the following result for the convergence of ss-local-sgd in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary g.20. let assumptions of theorem g.10 hold with  $\mu = 0$ . then for  $q = p$ ,  $r = \lceil 1 / p \rceil$  and

$$
\gamma_ {0} = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 \left(\frac {2 \mathcal {l}}{n} + l\right)}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 \sqrt {2 l (1 - p) \left((2 + p) l + p \mathcal {l} + \frac {(2 + p) (2 \mathcal {l} / r + l)}{(1 - q)}\right)}} \right\},
$$

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {p ^ {3} r _ {0} ^ {2}}{2 5 6 l (1 - p) (2 + p) ^ {2} {\bf e} [ \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} ]}}, \sqrt {\frac {n r _ {0} ^ {2}}{2 \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {p r _ {0} ^ {2}}{3 2 l (1 - p) (1 + ^ {3 2 (2 + p)} / 3) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2} k}} \right\},
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$ , we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^{k}) - f(x^{*})\right]$  is of the order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l + ^ {p \mathcal {l}} / n + \sqrt {p (1 - p) \mathcal {l} l}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{l (1 - p) \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} ] r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{p k} + \sqrt {\frac {\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2}}{n k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l r _ {0} ^ {4} (1 - p) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}}{p ^ {1 / 3} k ^ {2 / 3}}\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case ss-local-sgd requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l + p \mathcal {l} / n + \sqrt {p (1 - p) \mathcal {l} l}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2} + \sqrt [ 3 ]{l (1 - p) \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} ] r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{p \varepsilon} + \frac {\sigma_ {*} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2}}{n \varepsilon^ {2}} + \frac {r _ {0} ^ {2} \sqrt {l (1 - p) \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}}{p ^ {1 / 2} \varepsilon^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node (in expectation) and  $1/p$  times less communication rounds.

remark g.4. to get the rate from tbl. 4 it remains to apply the following inequality:

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} \right] \quad = \quad \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {0}} \left[ \left\| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {0}} \left(x ^ {0}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\stackrel {(1 3 9)} {=} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i}} ^ {0} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\bar {\xi} _ {i} ^ {0}} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {0}) \| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\stackrel {(1 9)} {\leq} \qquad 2 l (f (x ^ {0}) - f (x ^ {*})) + \frac {1}{n r ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {r} \mathbf {e} _ {\overline {{\xi}} _ {i, j} ^ {0}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\overline {{\xi}} _ {i, j} ^ {0}} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {0}) \| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad 2 l (f (x ^ {0}) - f (x ^ {*})) + \frac {1}{n r} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 6)} {\leq} \quad 2 l \left(f \left(x ^ {0}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + \frac {2}{n r} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {0}\right) - \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n r} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {\xi_ {i}} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {\xi_ {i}} (x ^ {*}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l l} r = \lceil 1 / p \rceil , (8 6) \\ \leq & 2 \left(l + 2 p \mathcal {l}\right) \left(f \left(x ^ {0}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) + 2 p \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}. \end{array}
$$

# g.5 s*-local-sgd*

in this section we present doubly idealized algorithm for solving problem (1)+(3). specifically, we choose  $b_{i}^{k}$  to the optimal shift  $\nabla f_{i}(x^{*})$  as per case ii, while  $a_{i}^{k}$  is selected as sgd-star gradient estimator [9], i.e.,

$$
a _ {i} ^ {k} = \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (x ^ {*}) + \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}), \qquad b _ {i} ^ {k} = \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}).
$$

note that now  $a_i^k$  serves as an ambitious target for the local variance reduced estimators, while  $b_i^k$  serves as an ambitious goal for the local shift. the resulting instance of (4) is presented as algorithm 5 and called star-shifted local-sgd-star (s*-local-sgd*).

let us next provide the details on the convergence rate. in order to do so, let us identify the parameters of assumption 4.1.

lemma g.12. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth and  $f_{i,j}$  be convex and  $\max l_{ij}$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ ,  $j \in [m]$ .

algorithm 5 s*-local-sgd*  
require: learning rate  $\gamma >0$  , initial vector  $x^0\in \mathbb{r}^d$  , communication period  $\tau \geq 1$    
1: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do   
2: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do   
3: set  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{i,j_{i}}(x_{i}^{k}) - \nabla f_{i,j_{i}}(x_{*})$  where  $1\le j_{i}\le m$  is sampled independently from all nodes   
4: if  $k + 1$  mod  $\tau = 0$  then   
5:  $x_{i}^{k + 1} = x^{k + 1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\left(x_{i}^{k} - \gamma g_{i}^{k}\right)$  ▷ averaging   
6: else   
7:  $x_{i}^{k + 1} = x_{i}^{k} - \gamma g_{i}^{k}$  ▷ local update   
8: end if   
9: end for   
10: end for

then for all  $k\geq 0$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right), \tag {119}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 4 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right], \tag {120}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 4 \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 l \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right], \tag {121}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \leq 4 \left(\frac {\max  l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 l \left(\frac {\max  l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right]. \tag {122}
$$

proof. first of all,

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left(\nabla f _ {i, j} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i, j} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right)
$$

and, in particular,  $\bar{g}_i^k = \mathbf{e}_k\left[g_i^k\right] = \nabla f_i(x_i^k) - \nabla f_i(x^*)$ . using this we derive

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right]
$$

$$
\stackrel {(1 9)} {\leq} \frac {2 l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \right] \stackrel {(6 3)} {\leq} 4 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right]
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {\leq} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \\ = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i, j} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i, j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2 \max  l _ {i j}}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 3)} {\leq} \quad 4 \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} [ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) ] + 2 l \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ]. \tag {123} \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, due to the independence of  $j_1,j_2,\ldots ,j_n$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {(1 3 9) _ {\underline {{\underline {{=}}}}} (1 4 0)} {=} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (x _ {*}) - (\nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}))\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left(\nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*})\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ = \quad \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} \left(x _ {*}\right) - \left(\nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right)\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {1}{n ^ {2} m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \| \nabla f _ {i, j} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i, j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} + \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 2 3), (6 3)} {\leq} \quad 4 \left(\frac {\max  l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + 2 l \left(\frac {\max  l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/8af76923c699754d90b2f2452d31babf934d493d99eff4945e10cc2da2376df1.jpg)

using corollary e.1 we obtain the following theorem.

theorem g.11. assume that  $f_{i}(x)$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex and  $l$ -smooth and  $f_{i,j}$  is convex and  $\max l_{ij}$ -smooth for every  $i \in [n]$ ,  $j \in [m]$ . then s*-local-sgd* satisfies assumption e.1 with

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widetilde {a} = 2 l, \quad \widehat {a} = 2 \max l _ {i j}, \quad \widetilde {b} = \widehat {b} = 0, \quad \widetilde {f} = 2 l ^ {2}, \quad \widehat {f} = 2 l \max l _ {i j}, \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = \widehat {d} _ {1} = 0, \\ a ^ {\prime} = 2 \left(\frac {\operatorname* {m a x} l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right), \quad b ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad f ^ {\prime} = 2 l \left(\frac {\operatorname* {m a x} l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right), \\ d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = 0, \quad \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \equiv 0, \quad \rho = 1, \quad c = 0, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \quad h = 0, \quad d _ {3} = 0 \\ \end{array}
$$

under assumption that

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{4 \left(\frac {\max  l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {e l (\tau - 1) (l (\tau - 1) + \max  l _ {i j})}} \right\}.
$$

moreover, for  $\mu > 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\bar {x} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq (1 - \gamma \mu) ^ {k} \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma}
$$

and when  $\mu = 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\gamma k}.
$$

the theorem above together with lemma i.2 implies the following result.

corollary g.21. let assumptions of theorem g.11 hold with  $\mu > 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 \left(\frac {\max l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {e l (\tau - 1) (l (\tau - 1) + \max l _ {i j})}} \right\}
$$

and for all  $k \geq 1$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\left(l \tau + \max _ {i j} l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max l _ {i j}}\right) \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \exp \left(- \frac {\mu}{l \tau + \max l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max l _ {i j}}} k\right)\right).
$$

that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right] \leq \varepsilon$  in this case s*-local-sgd* requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\left(\frac {l \tau}{\mu} + \frac {\max  l _ {i j}}{n \mu} + \frac {\sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max  l _ {i j}}}{\mu}\right) \log \frac {\left(l \tau + \max  l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max  l _ {i j}}\right) \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

next, we derive the following result for the convergence of s\*-local-sgd\* in the case when  $\mu = 0$

corollary g.22. let assumptions of theorem g.11 hold with  $\mu = 0$ . then for

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{4 \left(\frac {\max l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right)}, \frac {1}{8 \sqrt {e l (\tau - 1) (l (\tau - 1) + \max l _ {i j})}} \right\},
$$

we have that  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  is of the order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l \tau + \max  l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max  l _ {i j}}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{k}\right),
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$  . that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]\leq \varepsilon$  in this case s\*-local-sgd\* requires

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l \tau + \max  l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(\tau - 1) l \max  l _ {i j}}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node and  $\tau$  times less communication rounds.

# g.6 s-local-svrg

algorithm 6 shifted local svrg (s-local-svrg) for minimizing local finite sums

require: learning rate  $\gamma > 0$ , initial vector  $x^0 \in \mathbb{r}^d$ , probability of communication  $p \in (0,1]$ , probability of local full gradient computation  $q \in (0,1]$ , initialization  $y^0 = x^0$

1: for  $k = 0,1,\ldots$  do  
2: for  $i = 1,\dots ,n$  in parallel do  
3: choose  $j_{i}$  uniformly at random from  $[m]$  
4:  $g_{i}^{k} = \nabla f_{i,j_{i}}(x_{i}^{k}) - \nabla f_{i,j_{i}}(y^{k}) + \nabla f(y^{k})$  
5:  $x_{i}^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^{k + 1}, & \mathrm{w.p.} p,\\ x_{i}^{k} - \gamma g_{i}^{k}, & \mathrm{w.p.} 1 - p, \end{array} \right.$  where  $x^{k + 1} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i = 1}^{n}(x_i^k -\gamma g_i^k)$  
6:  $y^{k + 1} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}x^k, & \mathrm{w.p.}q,\\ y^k, & \mathrm{w.p.}1 - q \end{array} \right.$  
7: end for  
8: end for

in this section we are interested in problem (1)+(3). to solve this problem we propose a new method called shfted local-svrg (s-local-svrg, see algorithm 6).

we note that our analysis works even when updates in lines 5,6 are not independent. moreover, in order for s-local-svrg to be efficient, we shall require  $q \leq p$ .

remark g.5. unlike all other special cases, the rate of s-local-svrg can not be directly obtained from the theory of the local stochastic solver described in section 4. specifically, we construct the sequence  $l_i^k$  using  $y^k$  in contrast to  $x_i^k$  used in section 4. while we could construct  $l_i^k$  from the local iterate sequences, setting it as the virtual iterates yields a tighter rate. we remark that such a choice is rather poor in general; we can implement it efficiently thanks to the specific structure of s-local-svrg.

lemma g.13. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth and  $f_{i,j}$  be convex and  $\max l_{ij}$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ ,  $j \in [m]$ . then for all  $k \geq 0$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right), \tag {124}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 8 l \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] + 4 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right], \tag {125}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq 8 \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] + 4 l \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right], \tag {126}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \leq 4 \left(\frac {2 \max  l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + \frac {2}{n} \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} ] \\ + 2 l \left(\frac {2 \max l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ v _ {k} \right], \tag {127} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} \frac{1}{nm} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \sum_{j=1}^{m} \left\| \nabla f_{i,j}(y^k) - \nabla f_{i,j}(x^*) \right\|^2 + \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \left\| \nabla f_i(y^k) - \nabla f_i(x^*) \right\|^2.$
