proof. first of all, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ g _ {i} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} _ {k} \left[ \nabla f _ {i, j ^ {k}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} \left(y ^ {k}\right) + \nabla f \left(y ^ {k}\right) \right] \\ = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left(\nabla f _ {i, j} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i, j} \left(y ^ {k}\right) + \nabla f \left(y ^ {k}\right)\right) \\ = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and, in particular,  $\bar{g}_i^k = \mathbf{e}_k[g_i^k] = \nabla f_i(x_i^k) - \nabla f_i(y^k) + \nabla f(y^k)$ . using this we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] \quad \stackrel {(1 3 6)} {\leq} \quad \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) - (\nabla f (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f (x ^ {*})) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 9), (1 3 9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {4 l}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \right] + \frac {2}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i} \left(y ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 3)} {\leq} \quad 8 l \mathbf {e} [ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) ] + 2 \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} ] + 4 l ^ {2} \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ] \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| g _ {i} ^ {k} - \bar {g} _ {i} ^ {k} \| ^ {2} \right] = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (y ^ {k}) - (\nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k})) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {\leq} \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} \left(y ^ {k}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 6)} {\leq} \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i, j} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {2}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \nabla f _ {i, j} \left(y ^ {k}\right) - \nabla f _ {i, j} \left(x ^ {*}\right) \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 9)} {\leq} \quad \frac {4 \max  l _ {i j}}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbf {e} \left[ d _ {f _ {i}} \left(x _ {i} ^ {k}, x ^ {*}\right) \right] + 2 \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 3)} {\leq} \quad 8 \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + 2 \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} ] + 4 l \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ]. \tag {128} \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, using independence of  $j_{1}, j_{2}, \ldots, j_{n}$  we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {k} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {(1 3 9) _ {\underline {{\underline {{\alpha}}}}} (1 2 4)} {=} \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (\nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (y ^ {k}) - (\nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k}))) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ = \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) \right\| ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} {\bf e} \left[ | | (\nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j _ {i}} (y ^ {k}) - (\nabla f _ {i} (x _ {i} ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k}))) | | ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(6 8), (1 2 8)} {\leq} \quad 4 \left(\frac {2 \max l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] + \frac {2}{n} \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} ] + 2 l \left(\frac {2 \max l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right) \mathbf {e} [ v _ {k} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

lemma g.14. let  $f_{i}$  be convex and  $l$ -smooth and  $f_{i,j}$  be convex and  $\max l_{ij}$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ ,  $j \in [m]$ . then for all  $k \geq 0$

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \right] \leq (1 - q) \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} \right] + 2 \left(l + \max  l _ {i j}\right) q \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right] \tag {129}
$$

where  $\sigma_k^2 \stackrel{def}{=} \frac{1}{nm} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \sum_{j=1}^{m} \left\| \nabla f_{i,j}(y^k) - \nabla f_{i,j}(x^*) \right\|^2 + \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^{n} \left\| \nabla f_i(y^k) - \nabla f_i(x^*) \right\|^2.$

proof. first of all, we introduce new notations:

$$
\sigma_ {k, 1} ^ {2} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i, j} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2}, \quad \sigma_ {k, 2} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2}.
$$

secondly, by definition of  $y^{k + 1}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1, 1} ^ {2} \mid x _ {1} ^ {k}, \ldots , x _ {n} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1 - q}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i, j} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {q}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i, j} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 9)} {\leq} \quad (1 - q) \sigma_ {k, 1} ^ {2} + 2 q \max  l _ {i j} (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})), \\ \end{array}
$$

hence

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1, 1} ^ {2} \right] \leq (1 - q) \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k, 1} ^ {2} \right] + 2 q \max  l _ {i j} \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right]. \tag {130}
$$

next, the definition of  $y^{k + 1}$  implies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1, 2} ^ {2} \mid x _ {1} ^ {k}, \ldots , x _ {n} ^ {k} \right] = \frac {1 - q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} + \frac {q}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(1 9)} {\leq} \quad (1 - q) \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} + 2 l q (f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*})), \\ \end{array}
$$

hence

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1, 2} ^ {2} \right] \leq (1 - q) \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k, 2} ^ {2} \right] + 2 l q \mathbf {e} \left[ f \left(x ^ {k}\right) - f \left(x ^ {*}\right) \right]. \tag {131}
$$

finally, we combine obtained inequalities and get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1} \right] \quad = \quad \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1, 1} ^ {2} \right] + \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k + 1, 2} ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 0), (1 3 1)} {\leq} \quad (1 - q) \left(\mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k, 1} ^ {2} \right] + \mathbf {e} \left[ \sigma_ {k, 2} ^ {2} \right]\right) + 2 (l + \max  l _ {i j}) q \mathbf {e} \left[ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \\ = (1 - q) \mathbf {e} [ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} ] + 2 (l + \max l _ {i j}) q \mathbf {e} [ f (x ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) ], \\ \end{array}
$$

which concludes the proof.

![](images/e88f17c10830ee27a0a7db67b6fbab8d696e51c53e9c2bd0b2e4b9e7211753e1.jpg)

using corollary e.3 we obtain the following theorem.

theorem g.12. assume that  $f_{i}$  is  $\mu$ -strongly convex and  $l$ -smooth and  $f_{i,j}$  is convex and  $\max l_{ij}$ -smooth for all  $i \in [n]$ ,  $j \in [m]$ . then s-local-svrg satisfies assumption e.1 with

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widetilde {a} = 4 l, \quad \widehat {a} = 4 \max l _ {i j}, \quad \widetilde {b} = \widehat {b} = 2, \quad \widetilde {f} = 4 l ^ {2}, \quad \widehat {f} = 4 l \max l _ {i j} \quad \widetilde {d} _ {1} = \widehat {d} _ {1} = 0, \\ a ^ {\prime} = \frac {4 \max l _ {i j}}{n} + 2 l, \quad b ^ {\prime} = \frac {2}{n}, \quad f ^ {\prime} = 2 l \left(\frac {2 \max l _ {i j}}{n} + l\right), \quad d _ {1} ^ {\prime} = 0, \\ \sigma_ {k} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i, j} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i, j} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| \nabla f _ {i} (y ^ {k}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2}, \\ \rho = q, \quad c = (l + \max l _ {i j}) q, \quad g = 0, \quad d _ {2} = 0, \quad h = \frac {2 5 6 (1 - p ^ {2}) (2 + q) \gamma^ {2}}{3 p ^ {2} q}, \quad d _ {3} = 0 \\ \end{array}
$$

under assumption that

$$
\gamma \leq \min  \left\{\frac {1}{5 6 \max  l _ {i j} / 3 n + 4 l + 3 2 l / 3 n}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 \sqrt {2 l (1 - p) \left(l (2 + p) + p \max  l _ {i j} + ^ {4 (l + \max  l _ {i j}) (1 + p) / (1 - q)}\right)}} \right\}.
$$

moreover, for  $\mu > 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \left(1 - \min \left\{\gamma \mu , \frac {q}{4} \right\}\right) ^ {k} \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1 6 \gamma^ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}{n q} + \frac {1 0 2 4 l (1 - p ^ {2}) (2 + q) \gamma^ {3} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}{3 p ^ {2} q}}{\gamma}
$$

and when  $\mu = 0$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] \leq \frac {2 \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1 6 \gamma^ {2} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}{n q} + \frac {1 0 2 4 l (1 - p ^ {2}) (2 + q) \gamma^ {3} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}{3 p ^ {2} q}}{\gamma k}.
$$

the theorem above together with lemma i.2 implies the following result.

corollary g.23. let assumptions of theorem g.12 hold with  $\mu > 0$ . then for  $q = 1/m$ ,  $m \geq 1/p$ ,

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\frac {1}{5 6 \max l _ {i j} / 3 n + 4 l + ^ {3 2 l} / 3 n}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 \sqrt {2 l (1 - p) (l (2 + p) + p \max l _ {i j} + ^ {4 (l + \max l _ {i j}) (1 + p) / (1 - q))}} \right\}
$$

and for all  $k \geq 1$  we have  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^k) - f(x^*)\right]$  of order

$$
\mathcal {o} \left(\left(\frac {l}{p} + \frac {\max l _ {i j}}{n} + \frac {\sqrt {(1 - p) l \max l _ {i j}}}{p}\right) \phi^ {0} \exp \left(- \min \left\{\frac {\mu}{\frac {l}{p} + \frac {\max l _ {i j}}{n} + \frac {\sqrt {(1 - p) l \max l _ {i j}}}{p}}, \frac {1}{m} \right\} k\right)\right),
$$

where  $\phi^0 = 2\| x^0 -x^*\| ^2 +\frac{16\gamma^2\sigma_0^2}{nq} +\frac{1024l(1 - p^2)(2 + q)\gamma^3\sigma_0^2}{3p^2q}$ . that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^{k}) - f(x^{*})\right]\leq \varepsilon$  in this case s-local-svrg requires

$$
k = \mathcal {o} \left(\left(m + \frac {l}{p \mu} + \frac {\max l _ {i j}}{n \mu} + \frac {\sqrt {(1 - p) l \max l _ {i j}}}{p \mu}\right) \log \frac {\left(\frac {l}{p} + \frac {\max l _ {i j}}{n} + \frac {\sqrt {(1 - p) l \max l _ {i j}}}{p}\right) \phi^ {0}}{\varepsilon}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node (in expectation) and  $1/p$  times less communication rounds.

that is, s-local-svrg is the first implementable linearly converging stochastic method with local updates with a convergence guarantee in terms of the number of communications that is not worse than that of gd even in the arbitrary heterogeneous data regime.

next, we derive the following result for the convergence of s-local-svrg in the case when  $\mu = 0$ .

corollary g.24. let assumptions of theorem g.12 hold with  $\mu = 0$ . then for  $q = 1 / m$ ,  $m \geq 1 / p$  and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \gamma_ {0} = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{5 6 \max  l _ {i j} / 3 n + 4 l + ^ {3 2 l} / 3 n}, \frac {p \sqrt {3}}{3 2 \sqrt {2 l (1 - p) (l (2 + p) + p \max  l _ {i j} + ^ {4 (l + \max  l _ {i j}) (1 + p) / (1 - q))}} \right\}, \\ \gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {n r _ {0} ^ {2}}{8 m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {3 p ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2}}{5 1 2 l (1 - p ^ {2}) (2 m + 1) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2}}} \right\} \\ \end{array}
$$

we have

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ f (\overline {{x}} ^ {k}) - f (x ^ {*}) \right] = \mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l + ^ {p} \max l _ {i j} / n + \sqrt {(1 - p) l \max l _ {i j}}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{p k} + \frac {\sqrt {m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n} k} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{p ^ {2 / 3} k}\right),
$$

where  $r_0 = \| x^0 -x^*\|$ . that is, to achieve  $\mathbf{e}\left[f(\overline{x}^{k}) - f(x^{*})\right]\leq \varepsilon$  in this case s-local-svrg requires

$$
k = \mathcal {o} \left(\frac {\left(l + ^ {p \operatorname* {m a x}} l _ {i j} / _ {n} + \sqrt {(1 - p) l \operatorname* {m a x} l _ {i j}}\right) r _ {0} ^ {2}}{p \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt {m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {2}}}{\sqrt {n} \varepsilon} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{l m \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} r _ {0} ^ {4}}}{p ^ {2 / 3} \varepsilon}\right)
$$

iterations/oracle calls per node (in expectation) and  $1/p$  times less communication rounds.

remark g.6. to get the rate from tbl. 4 it remains to apply the following inequality:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{n m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {m} \left\| \nabla f _ {i, j} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i, j} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\| \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {0}) - \nabla f _ {i} (x ^ {*}) \right\| ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(6)} {\leq} 2 \left(\max l _ {i j} ^ {2} + l ^ {2}\right) \| x ^ {0} - x ^ {*} \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

# h basic facts

for all  $a, b, x_1, \ldots, x_n \in \mathbb{r}^d$ ,  $\beta > 0$  and  $p \in (0,1]$  the following inequalities hold

$$
\langle a, b \rangle \leq \frac {\| a \| ^ {2}}{2 \beta} + \frac {\beta \| b \| ^ {2}}{2}, \tag {132}
$$

$$
\langle a - b, a + b \rangle = \| a \| ^ {2} - \| b \| ^ {2}, \tag {133}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{2} \| a \| ^ {2} - \| b \| ^ {2} \leq \| a + b \| ^ {2}, \tag {134}
$$

$$
\left\| a + b \right\| ^ {2} \leq (1 + \beta) \left\| a \right\| ^ {2} + (1 + 1 / \beta) \| b \| ^ {2}, \tag {135}
$$

$$
\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} x _ {n} \right\| ^ {2} \leq n \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| x _ {i} \| ^ {2}, \tag {136}
$$

$$
\left(1 - \frac {p}{2}\right) ^ {- 1} \leq 1 + p, \tag {137}
$$

$$
\left(1 + \frac {p}{2}\right) (1 - p) \leq 1 - \frac {p}{2}. \tag {138}
$$

variance decomposition. for a random vector  $\xi \in \mathbb{r}^d$  and any deterministic vector  $x \in \mathbb{r}^d$ , the variance of  $\xi$  can be decomposed as

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \| \xi - \mathbf {e} [ \xi ] \| ^ {2} \right] = \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \xi - x \| ^ {2} \right] - \| \mathbf {e} [ \xi ] - x \| ^ {2} \tag {139}
$$

tower property of mathematical expectation. for random variables  $\xi, \eta \in \mathbb{r}^d$  we have

$$
\mathbf {e} [ \xi ] = \mathbf {e} [ \mathbf {e} [ \xi \mid \eta ] ] \tag {140}
$$

under assumption that all expectations in the expression above are well-defined.

# i technical lemmas

we now present a key technical lemma enabling our analysis. this is a refined version of lemma 14 from [46].

lemma i.1 (see also lemma 14 from [46]). for any  $\tau$  random vectors  $\xi_1, \ldots, \xi_\tau \in \mathbb{r}^d$  such that for all  $t = 2, \ldots, \tau$  random vector  $\xi_t$  depends on  $\xi_1, \ldots, \xi_{t-1}$  and does not depend on  $\xi_{t+1}, \ldots, \xi_\tau$  the following inequality holds

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \xi_ {t} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \leq e \tau \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {e} _ {t} [ \xi_ {t} ] \| ^ {2} \right] + e \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \xi_ {t} - \mathbf {e} _ {t} [ \xi_ {t} ] \| ^ {2} \right], \tag {141}
$$

where  $\mathbf{e}_t[\cdot ]$  denotes the conditional expectation  $\mathbf{e}[\cdot |\xi_{t - 1},\dots ,\xi_1]$

proof. first of all, if  $\tau = 1$  then (141) immediately follows from variance decomposition (139). otherwise  $(\tau > 1)$  for all  $l = 1, \ldots, \tau$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {e} _ {l} \left[ \left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {l} \xi_ {t} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \stackrel {(1 3 9)} {=} \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {l} [ \xi_ {l} ] + \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {l - 1} \xi_ {t} \right\| ^ {2} + \mathbf {e} _ {l} \left[ \| \xi_ {l} - \mathbf {e} _ {l} [ \xi_ {l} ] \| ^ {2} \right] \\ \stackrel {(1 3 5)} {\leq} \left(1 + \frac {1}{\tau - 1}\right) \left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {l - 1} \xi_ {t} \right\| ^ {2} + \tau \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {l} [ \xi_ {l} ] \right\| ^ {2} + \mathbf {e} _ {l} \left[ \| \xi_ {l} - \mathbf {e} _ {l} [ \xi_ {l} ] \| ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

taking full mathematical expectation and using tower property (140) we derive

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {l} \xi_ {t} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \leq \left(1 + \frac {1}{\tau - 1}\right) \mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {l - 1} \xi_ {t} \right\| ^ {2} \right] + \tau \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {e} _ {l} [ \xi_ {l} ] \| ^ {2} \right] + \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \xi_ {l} - \mathbf {e} _ {l} [ \xi_ {l} ] \| ^ {2} \right]
$$

for all  $l = 1, \ldots, \tau$ . unrolling the recurrence for  $\mathbf{e}\left[\left\| \sum_{t=1}^{l} \xi_t \right\|^2\right]$  we obtain

$$
\mathbf {e} \left[ \left\| \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \xi_ {t} \right\| ^ {2} \right] \leq \tau \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \left(1 + \frac {1}{\tau - 1}\right) ^ {\tau - t} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {e} _ {t} [ \xi_ {t} ] \| ^ {2} \right] + \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {\tau} \left(1 + \frac {1}{\tau - 1}\right) ^ {\tau - t} \mathbf {e} \left[ \| \xi_ {t} - \mathbf {e} _ {t} [ \xi_ {t} ] \| ^ {2} \right].
$$

since  $\left(1 + \frac{1}{\tau - 1}\right)^{\tau - t} \leq \left(1 + \frac{1}{\tau - 1}\right)^{\tau - 1} \leq e$  for all  $t = 1, \ldots, \tau$  we get (141).

lemma 1.2 (see also lemma 2 from [45]). let  $\{r_k\}_{k\geq 0}$  satisfy

$$
r _ {k} \leq \frac {a}{\gamma w _ {k}} + c _ {1} \gamma + c _ {2} \gamma^ {2} \tag {142}
$$

for all  $k \geq 0$  with some constants  $a, c_2 \geq 0$ ,  $c_1 \geq 0$  where  $\{w_k\}_{k \geq 0}$  and  $\{w_k\}_{k \geq 0}$  are defined in (12),  $\gamma \leq \frac{1}{h}$ . then for all  $k$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \text {e i t h e r} \quad \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{a \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / c _ {1} , a \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / c _ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{k} \leq \rho \\ o r \quad \frac {1}{h} \leq \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{a \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / c _ {1} , a \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / c _ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\gamma = \min  \left\{\frac {1}{h}, \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{a \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / c _ {1} , a \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / c _ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{\mu k} \right\} \tag {143}
$$

we have that

$$
r _ {k} = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(h a \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {\mu}{h}, \rho \right\} k\right) + \frac {c _ {1}}{\mu k} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right). \tag {144}
$$

proof. since  $w_{k} \geq w_{k} = (1 - \eta)^{-(k + 1)}$  we have

$$
r _ {k} \leq (1 - \eta) ^ {k + 1} \frac {a}{\gamma} + c _ {1} \gamma + c _ {2} \gamma^ {2} \leq \frac {a}{\gamma} \exp (- \eta (k + 1)) + c _ {1} \gamma + c _ {2} \gamma^ {2}. \tag {145}
$$

next we consider two possible situations.

1. if  $\frac{1}{h} \geq \frac{\ln\left(\max\{2, \min\{a\mu^2k^2/c_1, a\mu^3k^3/c_2\}\}\right)}{\mu k}$  then we choose  $\gamma = \frac{\ln\left(\max\{2, \min\{a\mu^2k^2/c_1, a\mu^3k^3/c_2\}\}\right)}{\mu k}$  and get that

$$
\begin{array}{l} r _ {k} \stackrel {(1 4 5)} {\leq} \frac {a}{\gamma} \exp (- \eta (k + 1)) + c _ {1} \gamma + c _ {2} \gamma^ {2} \\ = \quad \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(a \mu k \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\rho , \frac {\ln \left(\max  \left\{2 , \min  \left\{a \mu^ {2} k ^ {2} / c _ {1} , a \mu^ {3} k ^ {3} / c _ {2} \right\} \right\}\right)}{k} \right\} k\right)\right) \\ + \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {c _ {1}}{\mu k} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $\frac{\ln\left(\max\{2,\min\{a\mu^2k^2 / c_1,a\mu^3k^3 / c_2\}\}\right)}{k} \leq \rho$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} r _ {k} = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(a \mu k \exp \left(- \ln \left(\max  \left\{2, \min  \left\{\frac {a \mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}{c _ {1}}, \frac {a \mu^ {3} k ^ {3}}{c _ {2}} \right\} \right\}\right)\right)\right) \\ + \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {c _ {1}}{\mu k} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right) \\ = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(\frac {c _ {1}}{\mu k} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

2. if  $\frac{1}{h} \leq \frac{\ln\left(\max\{2, \min\{a\mu^2k^2/c_1, a\mu^3k^3/c_2\}\}\right)}{\mu k}$  then we choose  $\gamma = \frac{1}{h}$  which implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} r _ {k} \stackrel {(1 4 5)} {\leq} h a \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {\mu}{h}, \frac {\rho}{4} \right\} (k + 1)\right) + \frac {c _ {1}}{h} + \frac {c _ {2}}{h ^ {2}} \\ = \quad \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(h a \exp \left(- \min  \left\{\frac {\mu}{h}, \rho \right\} k\right) + \frac {c _ {1}}{\mu k} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\mu^ {2} k ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

combining the obtained bounds we get the result.

lemma i.3. let  $\{r_k\}_{k\geq 0}$  satisfy

$$
r _ {k} \leq \frac {a}{\gamma k} + \frac {b _ {1} \gamma}{k} + \frac {b _ {2} \gamma^ {2}}{k} + c _ {1} \gamma + c _ {2} \gamma^ {2} \tag {146}
$$

for all  $k \geq 0$  with some constants  $a > 0$ ,  $b_1, b_2, c_1, c_2 \geq 0$  where  $\gamma \leq \gamma_0$ . then for all  $k$  and

$$
\gamma = \min \left\{\gamma_ {0}, \sqrt {\frac {a}{b _ {1}}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {a}{b _ {2}}}, \sqrt {\frac {a}{c _ {1} k}}, \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {a}{c _ {2} k}} \right\}
$$

we have that

$$
r _ {k} = \mathcal {o} \left(\frac {a}{\gamma_ {0} k} + \frac {\sqrt {a b _ {1}}}{k} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{a ^ {2} b _ {2}}}{k} + \sqrt {\frac {a c _ {1}}{k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{a ^ {2} c _ {2}}}{k ^ {2 / 3}}\right). \tag {147}
$$

proof. we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} r _ {k} \leq \frac {a}{\gamma k} + \frac {b _ {1} \gamma}{k} + \frac {b _ {2} \gamma^ {2}}{k} + c _ {1} \gamma + c _ {2} \gamma^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {a}{\min  \left\{\gamma_ {0} , \sqrt {\frac {a}{b _ {1}}} , \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {a}{b _ {2}}} , \sqrt {\frac {a}{c _ {1} k}} , \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {a}{c _ {2} k}} \right\} k} + \frac {b _ {1}}{k} \cdot \sqrt {\frac {a}{b _ {1}}} + \frac {b _ {2}}{k} \cdot \sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {a ^ {2}}{b _ {2} ^ {2}}} + c _ {1} \cdot \sqrt {\frac {a}{c _ {1} k}} + c _ {2} \left(\sqrt [ 3 ]{\frac {a}{c _ {2} k}}\right) ^ {2} \\ = \mathcal {o} \left(\frac {a}{\gamma_ {0} k} + \frac {\sqrt {a b _ {1}}}{k} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{a ^ {2} b _ {2}}}{k} + \sqrt {\frac {a c _ {1}}{k}} + \frac {\sqrt [ 3 ]{a ^ {2} c _ {2}}}{k ^ {2 / 3}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

□

table 7: the parameters for which the methods from table 2 satisfy assumption 2.3/e.1. absolute constants were omitted. the meaning of the expressions appearing in the table, as well as their justification, is detailed in section 5. ubv stands for the "uniform bound on the variance" of local stochastic gradient, which is often assumed when  $f_{i}$  is of the form (2). es stands for the "expected smoothness" inequality [11], which does not impose any extra assumption on the objective/noise, but rather can be derived given the sampling strategy and the smoothness structure of  $f_{i}$ . consequently, such a setup allows us to obtain local methods with importance sampling. next, the simple setting is a special case of es when we uniformly sample a single index on each node each iteration.
